HPKE Envelope executed Auth modes and context binding and described recipient-key KCI, but did not execute that forgery or a transferable-signature comparison.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Execute one named signcryption construction beside the existing HPKE Auth baseline with both participant identities and application context bound.
- [x] Compromise a sender or recipient and compute the selected construction's insider/outsider confidentiality and authenticity outcomes.
- [x] Forward a valid object to a third party and distinguish transferable evidence from recipient-only authentication.

**Delivered implementation:** HPKE Envelope now executes the RFC 9180 §9.1.1 Auth recipient-key KCI forgery from recipient `skR` and public sender `pkS` without sender `skS`. Beside it, a versioned An–Dodis–Rabin-style multi-user Sign-then-Encrypt teaching profile uses separate X25519 and Ed25519 keys, signs a domain-separated length-framed tuple of sender, intended recipient, application context and message, and separately binds the same outer identities and context in Base HPKE `info` and AAD. Receiver APIs require independently expected context and pinned identity-to-key records. Nine learner cases execute honest delivery, outsider tampering, recipient compromise, sender signing-key compromise, combined compromise, forwarding, public signature evidence and replay; rejected plaintext and evidence remain quarantined.

**Verification:** [HPKE Envelope PR #31](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hpke-envelope/pull/31) merged as exact main [`fcf3b56c06e1`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hpke-envelope/tree/fcf3b56c06e1ef16b10b0db8e1b7fec60d295de8). Final-head [run 40178893409](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hpke-envelope/actions/runs/40178893409) and exact-main/deployment [run 40179028196](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hpke-envelope/actions/runs/40179028196) each passed typechecking, 258 unit tests, a passing unmutated baseline with all 19 security mutations caught, production build and all 27 Chromium functional/accessibility tests. The exact-main run deployed. Public `index.html` (7,519 bytes, SHA-256 `a785fd3b7550a5726ece908de1d807561425af8b535406af91ffd21913eca27b`), `assets/index-BP0y25b8.js` (109,616 bytes, SHA-256 `97a532ad90961e3091803d611ca6bed6fb96b9e45d011e76506d0b7e6be01b94`) and `assets/index-CObJQuzx.css` (11,037 bytes, SHA-256 `bb8e0776ad10b8d2f1549de903f0fd7f7e4240c654c87b290d07c43298fad7e3`) matched a fresh exact-main build byte for byte; the served bundle contains the nine-case completion, current HPKE-draft status, replay result and non-standardized-wire-format boundary.

**Next action:** Preserve the exact KCI, independent-context, pinned-key, plaintext-quarantine and evidence boundaries while the implementation queue advances to A-D-12 and repository-blocked S06 remains queued.

**Scope and limits:** The StE packet is a fixed teaching profile, not a standardized signcryption wire format or production protocol, and no claim is made that the generic paper's proof automatically transfers to this implementation. Recipient-key compromise exposes ciphertexts to that recipient but does not create a new valid sender signature while Ed25519 remains safe; sender-signing-key compromise permits new signed forgeries but does not by itself open recorded Base-HPKE ciphertexts. Public evidence shows only that a separately pinned key signed the exact disclosed fields. It does not prove ciphertext authorship, transmission, receipt, time, human intent or legal non-repudiation. A recipient can re-encrypt an unchanged valid signed payload, and the profile supplies no message identifier or replay cache. Public verifiability is not a property of every signcryption scheme.

**Audit references:** [Signcryption (original PDF pp. 5952–5963)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_156); [An, Dodis and Rabin, *On the Security of Joint Signature and Encryption*](https://eprint.iacr.org/2002/046); [RFC 9180](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9180.html); [RFC 8032](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8032.html); [active HPKE working-group Internet-Draft](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-hpke-hpke/).

**Pinned delivered source and comparison baselines:** [HPKE Envelope fcf3b56c06e1](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hpke-envelope/tree/fcf3b56c06e1ef16b10b0db8e1b7fec60d295de8); [Protocol Compose 5dd48e8f4abf](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-protocol-compose/tree/5dd48e8f4abf8d222985d1578da699c61c07b0ee); [Iron Letter e5326a46e859](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-iron-letter/tree/e5326a46e85923e95083baffbad57c9d68af3b16).

<a id="s06"></a>

### S06 — Distance bounding and relay attacks

**Priority:** P1 — next · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Device pairing and proximity.

**Target:** [`crypto-lab-distance-bounding`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding), live at exact main `4458307b57a02da4362cee5b799f20bdade03fd4`.

**Existing base:** Existing labs authenticate keys and model time/freshness; these do not establish physical proximity.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Authenticate an exchange successfully through a relay, then add a modeled unpredictable rapid-bit phase.
- [x] Vary propagation delay, processing delay, pre-guessing and tolerated errors and derive the resulting bound and failure cases.
- [x] Separate relay/mafia fraud from dishonest-prover distance fraud in the chosen protocol.

**Delivered implementation:** The lab executes the original Hancke–Kuhn verifier-nonce, one-fast-exchange profile. It derives `R0 || R1` from a shared key and fresh verifier nonce through a declared HMAC-SHA-256 PRF, executes one-bit unpredictable challenges, and models round-trip propagation plus declared prover/relay processing with exact integer/rational arithmetic. A domain-separated HMAC challenge/tag makes the ordinary authenticated relay control real; the timed full relay, pre-ask mafia attacker and dishonest key-holding prover remain separate. Current-run transcripts accompany exact ideal-model calculations for the one-session `(3/4)^n` case, threshold tails, partial pre-asking, repeated attempts, honest-noise false rejection and realized-table distance fraud. Predictable challenges, reused nonces, disabled timing, content corruption and over-bound inputs explicitly withdraw or reject the affected claim instead of preserving a stale verdict.

**Verification:** [PR #1](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/pull/1) merged independently corrected feature `43b6c3d11094` as `5f0b6a208fd2` with an identical tree; README-only [PR #3](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/pull/3) merged `28572e70b05c` as current exact main `4458307b57a0`, again with an identical tree. Exact-main [CodeQL run 40312351878](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/actions/runs/40312351878) and [quality/deployment run 40312352129](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/actions/runs/40312352129) passed 96 unit tests, 99.44/98.42/100/100 coverage, 20 learner-claim checks, 10 accessibility/reflow/keyboard checks and all 15 clean-baseline mutations. All three public files matched Pages artifact `12644478979` and a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the exact timing/probability arithmetic, independent ordinary-authentication control, actor-view separation and physical-measurement limits while the implementation queue advances to S11.

**Scope and limits:** Browser wall-clock measurements cannot substantiate nanosecond radio distance or physical proximity. The lesson is a deterministic protocol/event model, not a radio, UWB implementation, ranging device or general relay defense. The `3/4` term belongs to the stated ideal response-table/pre-ask model; physical-layer early-detect/late-commit effects and prover-nonce grinding are outside the initial executable profile.

**Audit references:** [Hancke and Kuhn, *An RFID Distance Bounding Protocol* (SecureComm 2005, author-hosted paper)](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~mgk25/sc2005-distance.pdf); [Avoine et al., *A framework for analyzing RFID distance bounding protocols*](https://doi.org/10.3233/JCS-2010-0408); [Security of Distance Bounding Protocols (original PDF pp. 5725–5727)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_62).

**Pinned baseline and delivery:** [Distance Bounding initial main e6e221b7fdea](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/tree/e6e221b7fdea453807b4813dc440ce31b65d29f3); [reviewed delivery main 4458307b57a0](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-distance-bounding/tree/4458307b57a02da4362cee5b799f20bdade03fd4); [DC-net quality-template main 8de5cb25042c](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-dc-net/tree/8de5cb25042cc70b991fe53211d85e3324bc9684); [X3DH Wire 2d2d5b13bcb1](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-x3dh-wire/tree/2d2d5b13bcb115b43a76feb4470a5ea1770bba62); [Time Trust 1578c9763ac5](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-time-trust/tree/1578c9763ac5aa15a343ee97a75be29eaddf2d79).

## Phase 4 — Construction and cryptanalysis depth

Build P2 lessons on existing mechanisms. Classical faulty-output DFA precedes ineffective-fault work; existing Trivium and Matsui extensions remain credited.

<a id="a-d-06"></a>

### A-D-06 — Classic block-cipher differential fault recovery

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Fault analysis.

**Target:** AES Anatomy extension.

**Existing base before delivery:** AES Anatomy exposed full AES-128/192/256 states and intentional layer-removal attacks. Biham Lens supplies differential cryptanalysis; Lattice Fault executes different fault mechanisms against lattice signatures. The prior AES Anatomy README and catalog listed seven acts and no correct/faulty-output DFA.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Inject a declared transient byte/round fault into otherwise correct AES and produce both correct and faulty ciphertexts.
- [x] Derive and filter last-round-key candidates using only the permitted observations and verify them on held-out encryptions.
- [x] Exercise recomputation/output checking and a changed fault model, with unsuccessful recovery reported accurately.

**Delivered implementation:** Act 8 injects a known-location, unknown nonzero byte fault after ShiftRows and before MixColumns in AES-128 round 9. It runs the existing AES implementation to produce correct and faulty ciphertexts, then tests round-10 key-byte candidates with `InvSbox(C xor k) xor InvSbox(C_faulty xor k)` against the fault propagated by the appropriate MixColumns coefficient. A second faulty output intersects each quartet's candidate set to one, the exercise reconstructs the full round-10 key, reverses the AES-128 key schedule and verifies the recovered master key on a held-out plaintext. A real fault injected in a different row but analyzed with the original coefficients yields zero survivors rather than a false recovery. Clean recomputation detects the one-sided modeled fault, while two executions with the same modeled common-mode fault compare equal; the page explicitly declines to present that check as a universal defense.

**Verification:** [PR #3](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/pull/3) merged as [`fffbc96eddeb`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/tree/fffbc96eddebefe2e45bce51890a35d0297570c1). The [pre-merge run 40007330722](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/actions/runs/40007330722) and [post-merge/deployment run 40008442510](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/actions/runs/40008442510) passed 39 unit tests, all 20 production-browser/accessibility checks and all 36 mutation cases. A separate 20-sample random-key/plaintext probe uniquely recovered every quartet within two faults. A fresh build matched the served `index.html` (SHA-256 `9e2f0bf6a97775b9a91a4570e86ee532730875cc3a67a2fbe57d61e2874b9533`), JavaScript (`assets/index-Gz0zo22g.js`, SHA-256 `e77e9df93837e96f1b21f214418c76b0475a9b445b7d96d99a5d13c39f552c70`) and CSS (`assets/index-DUU5TLjf.css`, SHA-256 `0c3b57dde8fb700160bd9270b385133a681a09a4eeff07184bcad9520a67d087`) byte-for-byte.

**Scope and limits:** The fault is a logical browser-state model at a known round/byte location, not a physical injection demonstration. The lesson is AES-128 and does not generalize its recovery count to every implementation or fault distribution. Layer removal is not DFA; RSA-CRT absence was not established and is not added as an accepted gap. The learner UI states the fault boundaries but leaves the full paper attribution in the README.

**Audit references:** [Differential Fault Analysis (original PDF pp. 1676–1682)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_1707); [Dusart, Letourneux and Vivolo, *Differential Fault Analysis on A.E.S.*, ePrint 2003/010](https://eprint.iacr.org/2003/010).

**Pinned delivered source:** [AES Anatomy fffbc96eddeb](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/tree/fffbc96eddebefe2e45bce51890a35d0297570c1); [Biham Lens 864ee0572f3e](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-biham-lens/blob/864ee0572f3e90496a240347a6094580a88a6318/README.md); [Lattice Fault 27a332b8d838](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lattice-fault/blob/27a332b8d83845632a09046dd76c51343fb20514/README.md).

<a id="e-k-06"></a>

### E-K-06 — Ineffective faults and leakage from accepted outputs

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Fault analysis.

**Target:** AES Anatomy follow-on, linked to Lattice Fault and Masked Core.

**Existing base:** A-D-06 now provides the classical faulty-ciphertext AES baseline in AES Anatomy. Lattice Fault already executes/simulates specified rejection-check and loop-abort faults, models timing/power leakage and states the limited protection of final verification. Masked Core and Ciphertext Mirror provide additional masking and detection context.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Apply a specified fault to a secret-dependent intermediate and retain only outputs classified as unchanged.
- [x] Recover a bit or rank candidates from accepted-output selection, then expose a distinct statistical-bias experiment if claiming SIFA.
- [x] Compare reject-on-detection and one scoped infective defense, including a fault model where the protection fails.

**Delivered implementation:** Act 9 clears a selected bit of state byte 0 immediately after first-round SubBytes, continues the existing real AES-128 implementation and compares the correct and fault-path ciphertexts exactly. Its seeded finite-sample experiment draws plaintext bytes and landing events, then gives the ranker only the inputs observable under the selected output policy and the target bit; it does not give the ranker the victim key, internal state or individual fault labels. A separate 2,048-row analytic census derives the declared six-of-eight population and is explicitly not sampled evidence. The secret-independent bit-flip control removes key-dependent selection. Reject-on-detection exposes absence; the deterministic teaching infection returns an output for every attempt and removes this ranker's selection signal when no classifier exists, while granting an exact clean ciphertext for the same plaintext restores the equality classifier and demonstrates the bounded protection's failure case.

**Verification:** [PR #4](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/pull/4) merged as [`1f0f6b86a2dd`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/tree/1f0f6b86a2dd5b631df13377b5e1a205e712a502). The [pre-merge run 40040111072](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/actions/runs/40040111072) and exact-main [post-merge/deployment run 40041039537](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/actions/runs/40041039537) passed 51 unit tests, all 21 production-browser/accessibility checks and all 44 mutation cases. The independent audit checked that finite samples execute the real fault path and equality classifier, that the ranker and null do not receive the secret, that the analytic census is labeled separately, and that infection/reference cases execute their claimed boundaries. A fresh exact-main build matched the served `index.html` (SHA-256 `13b8421118e1f55e03aaa0921069db5a07b52bb8b21f7ba9095f58dc05062cb4`), JavaScript (`assets/index-CWXSlZHY.js`, SHA-256 `156e93eafc66c5fdff4719176182aa3c702132d35cf1e81994c5a89c7334e040`), CSS (`assets/index-DsfsZ8HC.css`, SHA-256 `bf0f46a5df84323fa0119d3920a2937f657e4212a154696059b889aacf40e2ce`) and worker (`assets/attack.worker-BuntoPYK.js`, SHA-256 `fc863e3b4488665e75a3f8d82f052592d843a229fe986447b47ede91c3eaa6ae`) byte-for-byte.

**Scope and limits:** This is a chosen-input, one-byte logical teaching model with a declared profiled landing rate, not physical injection or a full multi-byte AES SIFA reproduction. The deterministic infection is not timing- or latency-oblivious, hardened, universal or the circuit defense analyzed by Daemen et al.; output-value, timing, common-mode, multi-fault and physical-oracle attacks remain outside the result. The exact-reference failure case deliberately grants an oracle the unprotected scheme would not normally provide.

**Audit references:** [Clavier, *Secret External Encodings Do Not Prevent Transient Fault Analysis*](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-74735-2_13); [Dobraunig et al., *SIFA: Exploiting Ineffective Fault Inductions on Symmetric Cryptography*, ePrint 2018/071](https://eprint.iacr.org/2018/071); [Daemen et al., *Protecting against Statistical Ineffective Fault Attacks*, ePrint 2019/536](https://eprint.iacr.org/2019/536).

**Pinned delivered source:** [AES Anatomy 1f0f6b86a2dd](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/tree/1f0f6b86a2dd5b631df13377b5e1a205e712a502); [Lattice Fault 27a332b8d838](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lattice-fault/blob/27a332b8d83845632a09046dd76c51343fb20514/README.md#L25).

<a id="a-d-05"></a>

### A-D-05 — Boolean combiner design: resiliency and algebraic immunity

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Symmetric cryptanalysis.

**Target:** LFSR Forge combiner-design extension.

**Existing base before implementation:** LFSR Forge demonstrated Geffe correlation recovery and Trivium/Berlekamp–Massey comparisons. Matsui Line measured linear approximations, and current AES Anatomy exhaustively computed S-box DDT/LAT properties. None supplied an editable Boolean-function audit with exact restriction and annihilator checks.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Edit a small Boolean truth table and compute balance, ANF degree and the full Walsh spectrum.
- [x] Fix subsets of inputs and compute correlation immunity/resiliency; search the declared bounded annihilator space.
- [x] Provide counterexamples showing that one favorable metric does not certify an entire stream cipher.

**Delivered implementation:** Panel 07 fixes the bound at exactly four inputs and recomputes every result from all 16 editable truth-table rows. It executes the Möbius transform for ANF and degree, all 16 Walsh coefficients, weight, balance, nonlinearity, correlation immunity and resiliency. A second independent enumeration fixes every nonempty input subset under every assignment and compares its conditional output count with the required independent distribution. The annihilator route tests every nonzero degree-at-most-two polynomial against both `f` and `1+f`—2,047 candidates per side, 4,094 checks total—and displays a nonzero witness plus its pointwise-zero product.

Three deterministic fixtures keep the criteria separate. Parity is balanced and order-three correlation immune but yields a Berlekamp–Massey recurrence that predicts a held-out 64-bit continuation without claiming recovery of four component states. An order-one-resilient function retains an order-two Walsh relation and allows a complete 7 × 15 R1/R2 pair scan; a distinct algebraic-immunity-two function retains a single-register relation and allows a complete 127-state R4 scan. Worker messages carry only copied public output and the fixed public register profile; the controller performs any hidden-state comparison separately, and a one-bit fixture stops on tied candidates.

**Verification:** [LFSR Forge PR #4](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/pull/4) merged as exact main [`7c6246133b9e`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/7c6246133b9ea5d2acda5ee1cddeada6e5b1e607); its feature and merge trees are identical at `d09da67ffe6b0d4fa7561c6b5942aa91261dcd22`. Final-head [run 40182270637](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40182270637) and exact-main/deployment [run 40182863297](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40182863297) passed five unit-test files with 32 tests, all 17 named clean-baseline mutations and all 17 hosted browser/accessibility tests; the exact-main browser gate completed in 50.4 seconds. The latter run's build job `121137427640` and deploy job `121138534192` passed, and deployment `7311559932` finished successfully as status `20538805495`. The public page returned HTTP 200 and contained the intended Boolean-analysis and counterexample strings. Its `index.html` (3,355 bytes, SHA-256 `2ab3c917727b6be85a08461e57247d0d7f4461c7a3ba4e0863efd03148faf47c`), main JavaScript (72,008 bytes, `3749d1a7585d4d3b50df907316857130bf091aadbba8eb59f397f1c392ff236b`), CSS (9,678 bytes, `dd2c2a895e828eda34a5cf26a74d60679dbe5ae3be791a89ac23b4f28ad56be5`) and three worker bundles (3,191/3,074/4,777 bytes; `b5c78d2296f00df2d408d99547d740f0b631b8827aab763cb607237fdc7bad27`, `c25a05e9c5171cea879b64646bc8fc506ee6343c05298b46cb50dcfa1c88e5bc`, `0e5373be45e89b803247e5d4da8fede56a3ef8c2d8d9e0c22029ca7c5b65aba3`) matched a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the exact independent-uniform, tiny-scan, public-worker and fast-algebraic-attack limits alongside A-D-11's separately scoped cube lesson while the queue advances to A-D-12.

**Scope and limits:** Walsh probabilities and fixed-subset independence assume independent uniform table inputs; the deterministic LFSR fixtures report their empirical agreement separately. The scans exhaust only 127 states or 105 state pairs at tiny public degrees and are not implementations or performance claims for production fast-correlation attacks. Algebraic immunity two is complete for four variables but does not exclude low-degree nonzero multiples or every fast algebraic attack. A finite tie, miss or leading candidate is an experiment outcome, not a general security certificate.

**Audit references:** [Siegenthaler, *Correlation-immunity of nonlinear combining functions for cryptographic applications*](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.1984.1056949); [Xiao and Massey, *A spectral characterization of correlation-immune combining functions*](https://doi.org/10.1109/18.6037); [Courtois and Meier, *Algebraic Attacks on Stream Ciphers with Linear Feedback*](https://www.iacr.org/archive/eurocrypt2003/26560345/26560345.pdf); [Meier, Pasalic and Carlet, *Algebraic Attacks and Decomposition of Boolean Functions*](https://www.iacr.org/archive/eurocrypt2004/30270469/finaleurocr.pdf); [Carlet, Dalai, Gupta and Maitra, *Algebraic Immunity for Cryptographically Significant Boolean Functions*](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.2006.876253).

**Pinned merged source and comparison baselines:** [LFSR Forge 7c6246133b9e](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/7c6246133b9ea5d2acda5ee1cddeada6e5b1e607); [Matsui Line e004db682cee](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-matsui-line/blob/e004db682cee90b73afd5f7c75f42a7cc20ce96a/README.md); [AES Anatomy 254a8069f781](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-aes-anatomy/blob/254a8069f7811e6b766199ef97f46c0c56a91c85/README.md).

<a id="a-d-11"></a>

### A-D-11 — Execute a cube sum and recover a superpoly

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Symmetric cryptanalysis.

**Target:** LFSR Forge toy-oracle companion section.

**Existing base before implementation:** LFSR Forge had seven wired panels. Its sixth panel ran real Trivium state clocking, 1,152 initialization steps, BM/forecast comparisons and a deliberately broken zero-initialization linear recovery; its seventh supplied A-D-05's Boolean-combiner audit. The Trivium panel explicitly said it executed only the zero-round linear attack, not a cube attack.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Evaluate every assignment of a public-input cube and display monomial cancellation in a tiny Boolean oracle.
- [x] Recover and validate each candidate superpoly, including valid cube C34 whose quadratic superpoly is not a maxterm and is therefore excluded only from the bounded affine solve.
- [x] Use independent valid equations to recover a toy secret and verify it with held-out observations.

**Delivered implementation:** Panel 08 defines an explicit degree-four polynomial over four public and three secret GF(2) variables. Preprocessing evaluates four complete two-variable cube faces for every one of the eight teaching keys—128 raw evaluations covering 72 distinct secret/public-row pairs—and applies a Möbius transform to each resulting truth table. It recovers C12 = k1 ⊕ k2 ⊕ 1, C13 = k2 ⊕ k3, C24 = k1 ⊕ k2 ⊕ k3 ⊕ 1 and C34 = k1k2 ⊕ k3. The interface exposes which master-polynomial terms survive, cancel or become zero, labels C34 as a valid cube with a nonlinear nonmaxterm superpoly, and sends only C12, C13 and C24 into the affine recovery.

Online recovery queries the three accepted cube faces under one controller-held key. Their rank-three equations and recorded sums 111 filter the eight teaching keys 8 → 4 → 2 → 1 to frozen candidate 110. Four disjoint held-out public rows produce fingerprint 1011 before a separate controller comparison. Dropping C24 stops with candidates 001/110; flipping C13 yields the wrong full-rank candidate 000 before held-outs reject it. Learner-visible work counts distinguish raw evaluations from distinct public rows, and rerunning preprocessing retires all prior online and failure-case verdicts.

**Verification:** [LFSR Forge PR #5](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/pull/5) merged as exact main [`66d95370b0a3`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/66d95370b0a3f5bb6641d349b5bdb1135b3ad8a4), with reviewed feature and merge trees identical at `1643c5a0e1d9519347807e5c1a353d3802a7c9ca`. Final-head [run 40185620598](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40185620598) and exact-main/deployment [run 40186149726](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40186149726) passed six unit-test files with 38 tests, all 23 named clean-baseline mutations and all 19 hosted browser/accessibility tests. Exact main deployed successfully, and the public HTML, JavaScript, CSS and three workers matched a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the toy-oracle/Trivium boundary, valid-cube/maxterm distinction and held-out verification. A-D-12's separately labeled historical A5/1 and irregular-clocking lesson is delivered below rather than folded into this cube-attack claim.

**Scope and limits:** This is a three-secret/four-public degree-four teaching oracle. Preprocessing enumerates all eight teaching keys; online recovery assumes repeated chosen-public-input queries under one fixed secret. Nonlinear-superpoly techniques are out of scope. The panel executes no Trivium state or round and makes no attack or performance claim against reduced-round or full Trivium. Its source-visible note reports an apparent constant-term inconsistency found by direct evaluation of the cited paper's introductory fixture; it does not call that an official erratum and instead uses an independently checkable oracle.

**Audit references:** [Dinur and Shamir, *Cube Attacks on Tweakable Black Box Polynomials*, ePrint 2008/385](https://eprint.iacr.org/2008/385).

**Pinned delivered source:** [LFSR Forge 66d95370b0a3](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/66d95370b0a3f5bb6641d349b5bdb1135b3ad8a4).

<a id="a-d-12"></a>

### A-D-12 — A5/1 and irregular clocking

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Stream cipher design.

**Target:** LFSR Forge extension.

**Existing base:** LFSR Forge teaches regular LFSRs, Geffe combination/correlation and current Trivium. Air Stream implements SNOW 3G, AES-CTR and ZUC cellular families. These are substantive stream-cipher activities.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Predict and execute majority/clock-controlled register advances and compare them with regular clocking.
- [x] Implement a separately labeled historical A5/1 mode with correct key/frame initialization and register distinctions.
- [x] Run a bounded known-state or guessing experiment and expose the assumptions changed by irregular stepping.

**Delivered implementation:** Panel 09 implements the reverse-engineered historical Briceno–Goldberg–Wagner definition with distinct 19-, 22- and 23-bit registers. All registers begin at zero; the first displayed Kc byte enters first with each byte least-significant bit first, the 22-bit COUNT enters least-significant bit first, every input bit clocks all three registers before XOR into bit 0, and 100 output-disabled majority clocks precede clock-before-output generation. Independently transcribed tests reproduce the archived program's post-key, post-COUNT and post-100 checkpoints plus both consecutive 114-bit bursts. The interaction exposes the pre-clock control bits, learner prediction and actual majority decision beside a copied regular-clock counterfactual.

The separate bounded state-completion exercise starts from the post-100 keystream state, discloses an extraordinary 52 exact state bits and enumerates the remaining 12. Thirty-two aligned known-keystream bits filter 4,096 completions to candidate 1425; 32 disjoint held-out bits verify the frozen result before a separate controller comparison. Sixteen observations leave 11 candidates, regular clocking yields no completion after two observations, and flipping one-based observation 29 selects candidate 1409 before 11 held-out mismatches reject it. The worker receives masked evidence rather than the controller state, and changed evidence, restart, cancellation, malformed replies and page exit retire stale verdicts.

**Verification:** [LFSR Forge PR #6](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/pull/6) merged as exact main [`c4ef2da4b33f`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/c4ef2da4b33fd08382a4309d9665745cb30bbda5), with final feature commit `d54a48be30dcfb635637ad146863f7a5f0d2144d` and the squash merge sharing tree `b5e4c25d6282f23cdfc2bc7da6065de30debc1fd`. Final-head [run 40192926530](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40192926530) and exact-main/deployment [run 40193796889](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/actions/runs/40193796889) passed 47 unit tests, all 33 named clean-baseline mutations and all 23 hosted browser/accessibility tests. Exact-main mutation artifact `12598544714` contains 33 unique cases with zero retries and matching restored hashes; its ZIP SHA-256 is `cec20af159b175919b97752a24b519a572553ac0d435bfc3399a925034704da3` and JSON SHA-256 is `72843200dace396989b49a58e2ba43b8176a014407d277140c91dd767c04547b`. Build job `121170192142`, deploy job `121173211399` and deployment `7313310897` / status `20545845058` succeeded. All seven public assets matched a fresh exact-main build byte for byte: `index.html` (3,383 bytes, SHA-256 `b84bbaf3eb5e74edf6341df77d19fd6652508730b07a5d3bae52d43561aef717`), JavaScript (115,970 bytes, `34629f08329a0ab3c28711cc2c91be1af2a99be26a69a3c0bcf5fed7082d0d1d`), CSS (10,998 bytes, `3c523c0df5e1326fbcd5a11e551bed26b94d703357f5387c991489c40cbdf5eb`) and four workers (3,191/3,074/4,672/4,777 bytes; `b5c78d2296f00df2d408d99547d740f0b631b8827aab763cb607237fdc7bad27`, `c25a05e9c5171cea879b64646bc8fc506ee6343c05298b46cb50dcfa1c88e5bc`, `57d8fbab9b3b32ca5e039e5eb71335a2f99e2e9c0f97abaea1305e9486129261`, `0e5373be45e89b803247e5d4da8fede56a3ef8c2d8d9e0c22029ca7c5b65aba3`).

**Next action:** Preserve the historical-definition boundary, exact bit/clock conventions, masked-worker separation, held-out verification and explicit non-attack claims while later roadmap work proceeds.

**Scope and limits:** The internal cipher is the reverse-engineered historical BGW definition, not public modern-standard text. ETSI documents the external Kc, COUNT and burst interface while referring the internal algorithm elsewhere. The state-completion exercise assumes an already aligned known keystream and disclosure of 52 exact post-warm-up state bits; its 4,096 raw completions are not proved reachable from Kc setup. It is not a published A5/1 attack, does not recover Kc, processes no radio capture and says nothing about modern cellular encryption. Rejecting regular clocking in this fixture does not establish that irregular clocking is secure.

**Audit references:** [A5/1 (original PDF pp. 111–114)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_332); [Clock-Controlled Generator (original PDF pp. 997–1001)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_337); [pinned BGW pedagogical C source](https://github.com/NSAPlayset/TWILIGHTVEGETABLE/blob/02f25aeade71a9addc06e8f9678742e04d25fd59/A5.1/C/A5.1.c); [Biryukov, Shamir and Wagner, *Real Time Cryptanalysis of A5/1 on a PC*](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-44706-7_1); [ETSI TS 143 020 / 3GPP TS 43.020, Annex C](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_ts/143000_143099/143020/11.05.00_60/ts_143020v110500p.pdf).

**Pinned delivered source and comparison baseline:** [LFSR Forge c4ef2da4b33f](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lfsr-forge/tree/c4ef2da4b33fd08382a4309d9665745cb30bbda5); [Air Stream 09529a8de623](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-air-stream/blob/09529a8de623f097d69bd397467acc2a21660a12/README.md).

<a id="a-d-14"></a>

### A-D-14 — Compose differential and linear cryptanalysis

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Symmetric cryptanalysis.

**Target:** Matsui Line extension.

**Existing base:** Biham Lens executes differential attacks and Matsui Line executes linear attacks. Matsui’s multiple-mask/four-joint-outcome and structural zero-correlation section remains separately mapped; A-D-14 adds an executed differential–linear composition on the same 8-bit toy-SPN family.

**Implementation status:** [Matsui Line PR #26](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-matsui-line/pull/26) squash-merged as exact main [`7a85fbf1e6f`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-matsui-line/tree/7a85fbf1e6f566f7b0b718cb93f5dd737950faaa); its tree is identical to the independently reviewed feature head `c66c4fda832f915d63a93d661d3a05b1436afaef`. Final-head run 40231832128 and exact-main/deployment [run 40232553089](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-matsui-line/actions/runs/40232553089) passed 122 unit, 32 browser/accessibility and 15 clean-baseline mutation checks. All five served files match a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Use one toy cipher to join a selected differential prefix to a linear suffix at an explicit intermediate state.
- Compute the combined statistic over chosen pairs and compare it with its stated heuristic and a failing-assumption control.
- Apply the statistic to bounded subkey ranking and verify the ranked result independently.

**Next action:** Preserve the fixed-RHS signed-path equation, honest 256-versus-16 candidate boundary, finite-sample/tie semantics, holdout separation, deterministic-sample qualification and master-key/full-cipher limits as Matsui Line evolves.

**Scope and limits:** Matsui's two-mask and structural-zero section remains separate. The new exhibit composes a fixed chosen-difference prefix and fixed linear suffix over a three-round toy, ranks observable final-whitening material and verifies a winner when fresh pairs remain. It does not recover the master key, attack the full four-round toy, reproduce DES, establish optimal masks or support a production-cipher claim.

**Audit references:** [Langford and Hellman, *Differential–Linear Cryptanalysis*](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-48658-5_3); [Biham, Dunkelman and Keller, *Enhancing Differential-Linear Cryptanalysis*](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-36178-2_16); [Lu, *New Methodologies for Differential-Linear Cryptanalysis and Its Extensions* §3.1](https://eprint.iacr.org/2010/025).

**Pinned delivered source and comparison baseline:** [Biham Lens 864ee0572f3e](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-biham-lens/blob/864ee0572f3e90496a240347a6094580a88a6318/README.md); [Matsui Line 7a85fbf1e6f](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-matsui-line/tree/7a85fbf1e6f566f7b0b718cb93f5dd737950faaa).

<a id="e-k-04"></a>

### E-K-04 — Hard-core predicates and Goldreich–Levin

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Security games.

**Target:** Hidden Bit extension linked to ElGamal Plain.

**Existing base:** Hidden Bit already executes IND-CPA/CCA/signature games, the PRP/PRF switching experiment, a GGM tree, a live DDH reduction and a newer multi-key rare-signal game. ElGamal Plain now computes a public quadratic-character leakage experiment.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Execute a guided sequence of adjacent hybrids that names each exact rewrite, assumption-based replacement and bounded bad event, then derives the displayed cumulative advantage rather than jumping from endpoints.
- Show an exposed/easily predicted input predicate under a separately stated one-wayness assumption.
- Compute the random inner-product construction for (f(x), r) and display the biased-predictor reconstruction argument.
- Distinguish finite enumeration from the asymptotic theorem and state the security-parameter assumptions.

**Implementation status:** Live at exact Hidden Bit main `7f143a4e9403`. The seventh exhibit has a typed adjacent `H0` through `H5` AES ledger, a symbolic AES-PRP term, the exact `q(q−1)/2^129` bad-event ceiling, real AES-128 and lazy 128-bit permutation/function oracles, the exposed-predicate construction and a Rackoff-style subset-XOR/majority reconstruction that receives only `y`, predictor access and an independent preimage verifier. Changing `q` retires the old bound; the decoder never receives `x`; reconstructed candidates are accepted only after recomputing `f(candidate)=y`; and the UI distinguishes a legitimate probabilistic miss from verified recovery.

**Delivery evidence:** [PR #16](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/pull/16) merged as `7f143a4e94038a534198c3f69af8d93ccedd86c1`; its reviewed feature and squash-merge trees are identical. Final-head run 40200388966 and exact-main/deployment run 40201417098 passed. Exact main reports 117 unit tests, 42 hosted browser/claim/accessibility tests and all 35 clean-baseline mutations killed. Pages artifact `12601163890` has ZIP SHA-256 `02f9e96f9e8539c172dcb7033039221592417520fb470e8c7ea7b955ec9f0cf0`; all 36 public files match that artifact byte for byte. Public JavaScript SHA-256 is `3b41d56aac6a9949c9e8eca237c43aba76f57f96f1b7edee9579b0cf278c1e7f`.

**Scope and limits:** Hidden Bit already teaches GGM, DDH reductions and security games; a small-domain experiment cannot demonstrate computational hardness.

**Audit references:** [Hard-Core Bit (original PDF pp. 2700–2702)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_412); [Goldreich and Levin's author page and original paper](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~/oded/gl.html); [Goldreich, *Modern Cryptography, Probabilistic Proofs and Pseudorandomness*, Appendix C.2](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~oded/PDF/mcppp-v2.pdf); [Bellare and Rogaway's code-based game-playing framework](https://eprint.iacr.org/2004/331); [Shoup, *Sequences of Games*](https://eprint.iacr.org/2004/332); [The Joy of Cryptography §2.4, adjacent hybrids and the three-hop maneuver](https://joyofcryptography.com/provsec/#sec.three-hop).

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [Hidden Bit 7f143a4e9403](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/tree/7f143a4e94038a534198c3f69af8d93ccedd86c1); [ElGamal Plain 251bf90ef747](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-elgamal-plain/blob/251bf90ef747f256d0e18f0fe987eadafa3f06da/src/qrGame.ts).

<a id="s09"></a>

### S09 — Target collision resistance as an order-of-play game

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Security games.

**Target:** Hidden Bit extension linked to Hash Zoo.

**Existing base:** Hash labs teach preimage/second-preimage/collision resistance; Hidden Bit teaches game order and security reductions.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- Run one small keyed-hash family under target-before-key and collision-after-key challenge orders.
- Enforce legal adversary moves in each game and demonstrate a strategy whose legality or success changes with the order.
- Explain the signed (key, hash) pair and target-collision statement without extrapolating toy collision counts to real hashes.

**Implementation status:** Live at exact Hidden Bit main `2f1ea68ada54`. The eighth exhibit runs independent target-before-key and key-first controllers over the 8-bit masked family `H_s(x)=h(x xor s)`, retires results when either setup changes, and refuses stale, repeated or out-of-order moves. In its planted-pair route, `h` is the identity except for the published inner collision `{0x0F, 0xF0}`. Exhaustive enumeration derives the displayed `2/256` target-first and `256/256` key-first counts; the key-first adversary shifts that pair back through the mask after seeing `s`, while the target-first adversary must commit before key sampling. The signed `(key, digest)` output and target-collision statement are visible beside explicit finite-demo, no-UOWHF, no-asymptotic-security and no-real-hash-attack limits.

**Delivery evidence:** [PR #17](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/pull/17) merged as exact main `2f1ea68ada54b88a89ccb0ae2c026eccabd37a83`; reviewed feature `2b899037ac7e987f2b9db672039328cb5cc36f78` and merge trees are identical. Final-head [run 40236124940](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/actions/runs/40236124940) and exact-main/deployment [run 40237685921](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/actions/runs/40237685921) passed. Exact main reports 148 unit, 51 hosted browser/claim/accessibility and 33 verdict tests; the final-head gate killed all 52 clean-baseline mutations. Pages artifact `12615453283` has ZIP SHA-256 `1b43693ac97080b69544814557c16cdc00c6892a50121578035cd28068702a78`; all 36 public files match it byte for byte.

**Scope and limits:** Target collision resistance is not established by empirical collision counts. Reuse E-K-04's guided-proof interaction vocabulary where it fits, but a generic hybrid scaffold does not satisfy this proposal's challenge-order state machine or separating strategy.

**Audit references:** [Universal One-Way Hash Functions (UOWHF) (original PDF pp. 6634–6637)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_624).

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [Hidden Bit 2f1ea68ada54](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hidden-bit/tree/2f1ea68ada54b88a89ccb0ae2c026eccabd37a83); [Hash Zoo e86dac25e78b](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-hash-zoo/tree/e86dac25e78bc6876e9fd644d1c6a0b823e06039).

<a id="a-d-09"></a>

### A-D-09 — Noninteractive designated-verifier proofs

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Proof verification models.

**Target:** Signature Gate extension linked to ZK Proof Lab.

**Existing base before delivery:** Signature Gate executed an interactive commit/challenge/response OR exchange for a signer or designated confirmer and demonstrated how a preselected-challenge recording could be simulated. Its proof/research limits were explicit, but it had no one-message designated-verifier proof or direct public-proof comparison.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Compute the paper's properly specified semantic disjunction using either the real statement witness or the designated verifier's secret.
- [x] Allow both prover and verifier to create accepting proofs and show what an external observer cannot establish about their origin.
- [x] Compare with a separately bounded public proof and the existing interactive confirmer exchange.

**Delivered implementation:** The fifth real-pairing chapter maps Jakobsson, Sako and Impagliazzo §5's `g, y_A, m, s` to the selected confirmation branch's `D_j, C_j, B_j, A`. One path uses the signer or confirmer witness; the other uses the secret behind a registered verifier key. Both feed one verifier. The chapter exposes the exact bijection from honest-prover randomness `(w,r,t)` to simulator randomness `(d,alpha,beta)`, creates an accepted verifier-generated transcript for a false statement, binds the complete statement, role, verifier key and caller context into its hash challenge, and refuses malformed, unregistered, replayed or cross-role objects. A separate ordinary Fiat–Shamir proof of the same two equations supplies the public contrast, and the older interactive exchange stays available as the challenge-order comparison.

**Verification:** [Signature Gate PR #15](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/pull/15) merged as exact main [`db6695e0f136`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/tree/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383). Exact-main runs [40243267066](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/actions/runs/40243267066), [40243267074](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/actions/runs/40243267074) and [40243267458](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/actions/runs/40243267458) passed the complete unit, real-pairing, mutation, independent Python, symbolic, browser/accessibility/reflow, production-build and Lean gates. Pages deployed artifact `12617637496`; its revision marker names the merge SHA and all 12 public files matched the artifact byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the construction/model boundary, exact two-origin distribution checks, false-statement demonstration, public-proof claim limits and hosted mutation gate while the queue advances through S06.

**Scope and limits:** The delivered route is the paper's concrete §5 trapdoor-commitment realization of “the statement is true, or I am the verifier,” not a generic Cramer–Damgård–Schoenmakers OR composition and not a hash of the existing interactive exchange. It implements Definition 1, not the Appendix's strong designated verifier, and is neither a designated-verifier signature nor a completed designated-confirmer-signature security result. The paper gives no §5 security theorem and the lab claims none. Its separate public comparison demonstrates publicly recomputable equations only; the lab supplies no proof-of-knowledge, soundness or unforgeability theorem for that object.

**Audit references:** [Jakobsson, Sako and Impagliazzo, *Designated Verifier Proofs and Their Applications*, EUROCRYPT '96, LNCS 1070, pp. 143–154](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-68339-9_13), especially §2, Definitions 1–2 in §3, §5, §7 and Appendix Definition 3. The encyclopedia entry remains useful secondary orientation but is not the construction source used for implementation.

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [construction, verifier, simulator and observer view](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/blob/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383/research/real-pairing/designated-verifier.mjs#L139); [learner controls and computed verdicts](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/blob/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383/research/real-pairing/app.mjs#L201); [source/model record](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/blob/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383/research/real-pairing/DESIGNATED-VERIFIER.md); [independent Python verifier](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/blob/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383/research/real-pairing/reference/verify.py); [clean-baseline mutation gate](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-confirmer-signatures/blob/db6695e0f136507572dcc10e652bff83ea357383/scripts/dv-mutations.mjs).

<a id="e-k-09"></a>

### E-K-09 — Knapsack trapdoors and the limits of worst-case hardness

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Historical trapdoors.

**Target:** LLL Break extension.

**Existing base before delivery:** LLL Break already stepped genuine small LLL/BKZ and attacked toy LWE embeddings; GGH Trapdoor was another lattice example. The prior LLL route mentioned knapsacks but did not execute their cryptosystem.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Generate a toy superincreasing Merkle–Hellman key, encrypt message bits and decrypt with the trapdoor.
- [x] Build the original Lagarias–Odlyzko Algorithm SV subset-sum lattice, invoke the existing LLL engine and validate recovered bits against the public sum and exact unimodular transform.
- [x] Show reproducible bounded success and failure cases while distinguishing plaintext recovery from trapdoor reconstruction and refusing to infer a density threshold.

**Delivered implementation:** Exhibit 6 generates a validated toy private sequence, modulus and multiplier, derives public weights, encrypts selected bits and trapdoor-decrypts the ciphertext. The attack receives only the frozen public weights, target and delta, materializes the original unscaled Algorithm SV basis for both the target and its complement, uses the existing LLL reducer and accepts a candidate only after exact coordinate, public-subset-sum and unimodular-transform checks. A visible seed/n/scale recipe makes browser-budget failure reproducible, and a bounded fixed-seed sweep reports counts without promoting them to a threshold.

**Verification:** Corrected [LLL Break PR #27](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/pull/27) merged reviewed head `525f9e34e8a0` as exact main [`3f7ca812858c`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/tree/3f7ca812858c87e0ad6d3c403c86c705dde3e16a); their trees are identical. Final-head [run 40254281402](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/actions/runs/40254281402) passed, and exact-main test [run 40254867941](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/actions/runs/40254867941) plus deploy [run 40254867984](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/actions/runs/40254867984) passed 24/24 knapsack checks, 2/2 publication controls, the production build, 29/29 browser checks, 2/2 accessibility/reflow checks and all 19 clean-baseline mutations. Pages artifact `12622431732` deployed, and all five public files matched it byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the original-basis, public-only, exact-candidate and no-threshold/no-trapdoor boundaries while the queue advances.

**Scope and limits:** Keep the construction historical and broken; NP-completeness does not prove security of the generated distribution, and this is not Shamir's trapdoor attack.

**Audit references:** [Merkle and Hellman, *Hiding Information and Signatures in Trapdoor Knapsacks*, IEEE TIT 24(5), 1978](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.1978.1055927), §II and equations (1)–(8); [Lagarias and Odlyzko, *Solving Low-Density Subset Sum Problems*, JACM 32(1), 1985](https://doi.org/10.1145/2455.2461), §§1–2 and Algorithm SV; encyclopedia orientation: [Knapsack Cryptographic Schemes](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_323).

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [key generation, encryption, trapdoor decryption and Algorithm SV](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/blob/3f7ca812858c87e0ad6d3c403c86c705dde3e16a/src/knapsack.ts); [learner exhibit and controls](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/blob/3f7ca812858c87e0ad6d3c403c86c705dde3e16a/src/main.ts#L322); [scope and primary-source record](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-lll-break/blob/3f7ca812858c87e0ad6d3c403c86c705dde3e16a/README.md#L69).

<a id="l-r-02"></a>

### L-R-02 — Parallelizable block-cipher MAC (PMAC)

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** MAC constructions.

**Target:** MAC Race extension.

**Existing base before delivery:** MAC Race already executed HMAC, AES-CMAC, GHASH and Poly1305, with CBC-MAC forgery and key-reuse lessons.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Compute PMAC block offsets, independent cipher calls, combination and final-block handling from the pinned 2002 Black–Rogaway definition.
- [x] Compare dependency work/span with CMAC using the same message and explicitly modeled scheduling.
- [x] Mutate content, position and final-block treatment and verify the resulting tags with a full-byte verifier.

**Delivered implementation:** Lesson 6 runs the byte-aligned, full-tag 2002 PMAC definition over AES-256, with official AES-128/AES-256 vectors, exact Gray-code offset recurrence, independently computed non-final AES lanes and distinct complete/partial final rules. Content, position and deliberately nonconforming final-rule tags are recomputed and rejected by the correct verifier. The adjacent CMAC comparison counts exact per-message AES work and selected-lane dependency waves while separating cached key setup and refusing a browser-speed claim.

**Verification:** [MAC Race PR #27](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/pull/27) merged reviewed head `858806a5153f` as exact main [`695391521cfd`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/tree/695391521cfdaf4aee0dc3814b1d324b3b1bbdf1); their trees are identical. Final-head [run 40254019238](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/actions/runs/40254019238) and exact-main/deployment [run 40254779759](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/actions/runs/40254779759) passed two publication controls, 106 unit tests, 39 browser/accessibility/cross-browser checks and all 24 clean-baseline mutations. Pages artifact `12621759091` deployed, and all four public files matched it byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the exact-variant, full-byte verifier, dependency-model and classical-query/Q2 boundaries while the queue advances.

**Scope and limits:** The implementation covers byte-aligned messages and full tags, not the paper's arbitrary-bit/truncation interfaces. A scheduling visualization is not a measured multicore speedup. The lesson gives ordinary classical MAC queries and explicitly does not implement the stronger Q2 oracle under which a published Simon-style forgery applies; PMAC is not presented as NIST-standardized.

**Audit references:** [Black and Rogaway, *A Block-Cipher Mode of Operation for Parallelizable Message Authentication*, EUROCRYPT 2002](https://www.cs.ucdavis.edu/~rogaway/papers/pmac.pdf), §§2–5; author-hosted [PMAC-AES-128](https://www.cs.ucdavis.edu/~rogaway/ocb/pmac128.txt) and [PMAC-AES-256](https://www.cs.ucdavis.edu/~rogaway/ocb/pmac256.txt) vectors; encyclopedia orientation: [PMAC](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_603).

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [PMAC core](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/blob/695391521cfdaf4aee0dc3814b1d324b3b1bbdf1/src/pmac/pmac.ts#L178); [dependency model](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/blob/695391521cfdaf4aee0dc3814b1d324b3b1bbdf1/src/pmac/schedule.ts#L25); [learner panel and boundaries](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-mac-race/blob/695391521cfdaf4aee0dc3814b1d324b3b1bbdf1/src/pmac/panel.ts#L9).

<a id="a-d-13"></a>

### A-D-13 — BBS pseudorandom bits and Blum-Goldwasser rewind

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Number-theoretic generators.

**Target:** Residue Gate extension.

**Existing base before delivery:** Residue Gate implemented quadratic residues, the Jacobi +1 challenge, Goldwasser-Micali bit encryption and Rabin roots/CCA factor recovery, but did not iterate a BBS state or recover a Blum–Goldwasser stream from its transmitted final state.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Construct toy Blum primes and a valid quadratic-residue seed, then compute repeated squaring and the specified emitted bits.
- [x] Encrypt using the generated stream and transmitted final state, then use the factors to rewind and decrypt.
- [x] Show state-disclosure and ciphertext-tampering consequences and state the valid seed/output-bit assumptions.

**Delivered implementation:** Experiment 6 samples a fresh unit by rejection or accepts an explicit teaching seed, computes both the BBS 1986 and HAC 5.40 bit conventions, encrypts with HAC 8.56 block indexing and transmits `x_(t+1)`, then uses the worker-held factors to derive the rewind exponents and recover `x_0` before replaying the stream. Current-run controls expose the same-seed XOR relation, current-and-future output after state disclosure, a complete public walk around the pinned toy cycle, exact ciphertext-bit malleability, non-QR refusal and altered-valid-QR acceptance. Fixed and two-state manual seeds are classified rather than receiving a distributional security claim.

**Verification:** [Residue Gate PR #1](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/pull/1) merged reviewed head `a36e190dda24` as exact main [`f20e6db42edc`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/tree/f20e6db42edc5d654c9934dda27855dd6e340dfa); their trees are identical. Final-head [run 40263223623](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/actions/runs/40263223623) and exact-main [run 40264103164](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/actions/runs/40264103164) passed 39 unit tests, typecheck/build, 25 browser claims, two accessibility views and all 40 clean-baseline mutations. Pages artifact `12626350669` deployed through deployment `7323506551`, and all four public files matched it and a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the published indexing, distribution, prime, authentication, public-only recovery and lifecycle boundaries while the queue advances.

**Scope and limits:** HAC 8.56 indexes its first emitted block one squaring later than both original papers; the implementation says so. The original BG scheme uses `p,q ≡ 7 mod 8`, while this route follows HAC's distinct `p,q ≡ 3 mod 4` condition and obtains probable primes from 40 Miller–Rabin rounds. Short-final-block handling is a teaching adaptation. Toy cycles, finite output and displayed statistics prove no general pseudorandomness or backwards-unpredictability result. Blum–Goldwasser is unauthenticated and malleable, QR membership is not authentication, and this construction is unrelated to BBS/BBS+ credential signatures.

**Audit references:** Blum, Blum and Shub, [*A Simple Unpredictable Pseudo-Random Number Generator*](https://doi.org/10.1137/0215025), SIAM J. Comput. 15(2), 1986, §§4 and 7; Blum and Goldwasser, [*An Efficient Probabilistic Public-Key Encryption Scheme Which Hides All Partial Information*](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-39568-7_23), CRYPTO '84, encoding and Algorithms A/B; [HAC Chapter 5](https://cacr.uwaterloo.ca/hac/about/chap5.pdf), Algorithm 5.40, and [HAC Chapter 8](https://cacr.uwaterloo.ca/hac/about/chap8.pdf), Algorithms 8.55–8.56, Example 8.57 and Notes 8.58–8.59. Encyclopedia orientation: [BBS](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_141) and [Blum–Goldwasser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_142).

**Pinned delivered implementation:** [BBS/BG core](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/blob/f20e6db42edc5d654c9934dda27855dd6e340dfa/src/bbs/bbs.ts#L108); [learner panel and boundaries](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/blob/f20e6db42edc5d654c9934dda27855dd6e340dfa/src/main.ts#L32); [isolated key-holder dispatch](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-residue-gate/blob/f20e6db42edc5d654c9934dda27855dd6e340dfa/src/worker/compute.worker.ts#L9).

<a id="a-d-07"></a>

### A-D-07 — Certificateless public keys and the escrow tradeoff

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Identity-based alternatives.

**Target:** IBE Gate extension linked to PKI Chain.

**Existing base before delivery:** IBE Gate exposed the PKG master secret and actually derived arbitrary identity keys, making escrow concrete. Its separate HIBE route bounded delegation without removing root escrow. The suite separately taught certificate-based public-key trust, but did not execute a PKI/IBE/certificateless ownership comparison.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Compute authority-issued partial keys and user-chosen secret contributions under a named certificateless construction.
- [x] Compare ordinary PKI, IBE and certificateless issuance on one flow and test what the KGC master secret alone reveals.
- [x] Separate permitted public-key replacement from an adversary possessing the KGC master secret.

**Delivered implementation:** Exhibit 7 executes three fresh routes for one identity. PKI verifies a P-256 CA signature over the identity, context and RSA public key before RSA-OAEP encryption. IBE uses the existing real BasicIdent implementation so the KGC can extract and decrypt. The bounded certificateless route follows Huang and Wong §4.2's `IBE.Enc(ID, message || MAC key) → PKE.Enc(user key, c1) → MAC(c2)` mechanics using the lab's BLS12-381 BasicIdent, RSA-OAEP-2048/SHA-256 and HMAC-SHA-256. Its capability-limited Type-I API receives a replacement secret but no target partial key; its Type-II API receives the target partial key but creates an unrelated user secret internally. Each rejects alone, the explicit combined compromise accepts, and separate displacement and tag-tamper controls reject.

**Verification:** [IBE Gate PR #25](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-ibe-gate/pull/25) was independently audited at exact feature head `106dee16a4b1`, squash-merged with an identical tree as exact main `6c0b3fbd9ebf`, and deployed. Exact-head run `40309202902` passed 50 unit tests, 20 hosted browser/accessibility checks and all 10 clean-baseline mutations. Exact-main/deployment run `40310063824` passed, and the public HTML, CSS and JavaScript matched Pages artifact `12645010926` and a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Maintain the named construction, adversary capability split, uniform public reject and theorem boundary as the surrounding IBE/HIBE lab evolves.

**Scope and limits:** This is a bounded mechanics adaptation, not an ad hoc claim of a new construction and not a production CLE scheme. Huang and Wong's generic theorem assumes IND-ID-CCA2 IBE, IND-CCA2 PKE and a strong one-time MAC; the reused BasicIdent is only IND-CPA, so the theorem does not apply. The Type-I and Type-II cases are one-shot capability demonstrations rather than full adaptive games with extraction or decryption oracles. The public API collapses failures to one reject bit, but JavaScript is not constant-time. Public-key replacement can still deny service, and an authenticated directory remains necessary.

**Audit references:** Al-Riyami and Paterson, [*Certificateless Public Key Cryptography*](https://eprint.iacr.org/2003/126), for the Type-I/Type-II model; Huang and Wong, [*Generic Certificateless Encryption in the Standard Model*](https://eprint.iacr.org/2007/095), especially §§4 and 4.2, for the executed generic envelope mechanics; [Certificateless Cryptography (encyclopedia orientation, original PDF pp. 929–932)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_314).

**Pinned delivered source:** [IBE Gate 6c0b3fbd9ebf](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-ibe-gate/tree/6c0b3fbd9ebf167491eedff70b1b7cb7fee5f26f); [PKI Chain baseline 0ee9ca34cecd](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-pki-chain/blob/0ee9ca34cecdc1d434f013fa07b23865c56c382b/README.md).

### A-D-08 — Direct anonymous attestation and controlled linkability

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Anonymous attestation.

**Target:** Attestation Gate advanced extension.

**Existing base before delivery:** Attestation Gate computed measured boot and real ECDSA P-256 signatures over quotes, checked freshness/evidence, and showed the importance of the AK/EK trust story and runtime limits. Anonymous credential and ring-signature primitives existed elsewhere, but the lab did not execute a DAA construction.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Compare a stable attestation-key quote with issuer Join and anonymous membership verification from one named DAA construction.
- [x] Compute same-basename and different-basename signing cases and display intended linkability.
- [x] Change credential/issuer/revocation state and verify acceptance without creating a universal device identifier.

**Delivered implementation:** The seventh tab executes the no-application-attribute specialization of Camenisch–Drijvers–Lehmann `Pi_DAA+` §5.1 over Type-3 BLS12-381: issuer Setup with its cross-group DLEQ proof, a nonce-bound Join proof of the platform secret, BBS+ credential issuance, randomized Sign/Verify, named-basename pseudonyms and Link, issuer rotation with explicit re-Join, modified-credential rejection and the construction's private-key rogue-list check. A separate adapter signs the existing PCR- and nonce-bound TPMS_ATTEST bytes with an empty `qualifiedSigner`, so the anonymous route does not carry the stable AK Name. The stable-AK comparison, same- and different-basename cases, issuer/credential/rogue-list changes and every displayed verdict are derived from the current run. [Join](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/protocol.ts#L222) · [BBS+ issuance](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/protocol.ts#L271) · [Sign](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/protocol.ts#L384) · [Verify](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/protocol.ts#L493) · [Link](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/protocol.ts#L621) · [current-run orchestration](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/lesson.ts#L150) · [TPMS_ATTEST adapter](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/daa/attestation.ts#L33) · [learner panel](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61/src/ui/panels/daa.ts#L85).

**Verification:** [PR #13](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/pull/13) merged reviewed feature head `7cf643e1d51a477f84117caac7970d1d84f7c5b1` as delivery merge `7e7608ba609e600b5c1e5a1bf7932def1fd9c25e`; their trees are identical. Independent protocol review found and the merged branch fixed context-reuse flaws in issuer proof nonces, Join proof randomness, credential-issuance scalars and signature proof randomness; affine credential forgery from repeated issuance randomness; invalid-proof/revocation ordering; imprecise Join and side-channel wording; and missing negative controls. [PR #14](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/pull/14) corrected only the Wesemeyer publication title. [PR #15](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/pull/15) then corrected four places that mislabeled Schnorr proof randomness as the witness; reviewed head `8f355c197c25` and current exact main `89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61` have identical trees, and the executable public assets remain byte-identical to the PR #14 deployment. Current exact-main [run 40277880488](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/actions/runs/40277880488) passed 247 unit tests, 66 claims tests (65 passed, one intentional desktop coarse-pointer skip), three accessibility/reflow drives, the production build and all 16 clean-baseline mutations. Pages artifact `12630499763` deployed through deployment `7325307975`; all three public files matched that artifact and a fresh exact-main build byte for byte.

**Next action:** Preserve the authenticated-Join/unique-admission prerequisite, named-basename-only boundary, exact CDL equations and separate measured-boot/runtime claim when extending this lab; do not present the adapter as TPM/ISO interoperability.

**Scope and limits:** Generic anonymous credentials or group signatures alone do not implement DAA. This bounded adaptation has zero application attributes and omits `bsn = bottom`, signature-based revocation, hardware execution and TPM/ISO ECDAA wire interoperability. The Join channel and unique-TPM admission rule are external assumptions; BLS12-381 is an algebraic teaching instantiation; JavaScript secret arithmetic is variable-time; all fixture seeds are public; and the page proves neither the paper's UC theorem assumptions nor post-quantum security. Publishing a revoked platform secret enables basename pseudonym calculation. Anonymous provenance does not prove runtime integrity.

**Audit references:** [Camenisch, Drijvers and Lehmann, `Pi_DAA+`, full version, IACR ePrint 2016/663 §§4–6](https://eprint.iacr.org/2016/663.pdf); [RFC 9380 hash to curve](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9380.html); [Wesemeyer et al., Formal Analysis and Implementation of a TPM 2.0-based Direct Anonymous Attestation Scheme, Appendix A.2 and §§3.5–3.7](https://ethz.ch/content/dam/ethz/special-interest/infk/inst-infsec/information-security-group-dam/research/publications/pub2020/eccdaaimp-asiaccs20.pdf); [Direct Anonymous Attestation (Encyclopedia of Cryptography, Security and Privacy, pp. 674–678)](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_1742).

**Pinned baseline and delivery:** [Attestation Gate pre-extension e78e756f4320](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/blob/e78e756f43202bf6ab3ea47d2013a9b805f9a07b/src/tpm/key.ts#L33-L39); [A-D-08 current exact main 89b2bb4cee6d](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-attestation-gate/tree/89b2bb4cee6dcdfa0dbbabdc6ad1c82163659e61); [Credential Veil f06bbcaefb28](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-credential-veil/blob/f06bbcaefb287e438d9d626581f417f0d0fc81f0/README.md); [Ring Sign a1a63b52c927](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-ring-sign/blob/a1a63b52c9274bef08c581abcfbc35f50803d0d4/README.md).

<a id="s07"></a>

### S07 — Visual secret sharing

**Priority:** P2 — depth · **Status:** Live · **Workstream:** Secret sharing variants.

**Target:** Shamir Gate companion section or compact visual-sharing lab.

**Existing base at start:** Shamir Gate provided polynomial threshold sharing, reconstruction, insufficient-share privacy and an encrypted AES vault, but no visual-secret-sharing construction. Exact Shamir Gate main `48a7a3ab9298` now adds a ninth Visual Shares tab without changing the existing expected Shamir or AES results.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [x] Generate shares under a specified visual scheme and reconstruct a black/white symbol using Boolean-OR stacking.
- [x] Compute one-share pixel distributions, pixel expansion and the resulting contrast.
- [x] Compare stacking with XOR and polynomial interpolation and reject a visually scrambled-only secrecy claim.

**Delivered implementation, 2026-10-26:** The exact Naor–Shamir 2-of-2 basis uses all six weight-two four-subpixel patterns. Every source pixel gets a fresh uniform rejection-sampled choice: white maps to the same block in both labeled shares and black maps to complementary blocks. Physical reconstruction ORs the actual displayed share matrices; a separately labeled digital XOR result cannot be mistaken for transparency stacking. The lesson derives `m = 4` and relative contrast `alpha = 1/2` from the basis, enumerates all 24 labeled-share, source-color and mask outcomes to establish equality of the complete one-share block distributions, and keeps polynomial interpolation in a comparison table rather than conflating the two constructions.

Mask reuse computes equal/complement relations between adjacent second-share blocks, fixed position-only scrambling preserves distinguishing black-pixel counts, and a missing share refuses reconstruction. Editing the source, resizing it or generating new masks retires every dependent result. The browser presents exact bit matrices alongside the visual grids and retains the source and both shares only as an explicitly omniscient teaching view.

**Verification:** [Shamir Gate PR #18](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-shamir-gate/pull/18), reviewed feature head `ba8832470181`, squash-merged as exact main [`48a7a3ab9298`](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-shamir-gate/tree/48a7a3ab9298edf708f5496159c39f2cdb9d3227). Exact-main [run 40329795463](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-shamir-gate/actions/runs/40329795463) passed 73 unit tests, two publication controls, the production build, all 14 clean-baseline mutations and 19 browser claim, accessibility and reflow checks, then deployed successfully. Pages artifact `12654776334` and the public deployment had byte-identical HTML, CSS and JavaScript.

**Next action:** Preserve the exact full-distribution proof, fresh-mask requirement, physical-OR boundary and visible operational limits while the unassigned implementation queue advances to E-K-07.

**Scope and limits:** This is binary 2-of-2 visual secret sharing, not general k-of-n, grayscale, color or progressive visual cryptography. It supplies confidentiality for one correctly generated share under independent per-pixel masks, but no authenticity, custody system, physical alignment mechanism or printing/opacity/scaling/rotation/dot-gain/gamma/registration model. The two shares reveal the source by design, and visual scrambling alone does not demonstrate perfect secrecy.

**Audit references:** [Naor and Shamir, Visual Cryptography, EUROCRYPT 1994, §§2–3](https://doi.org/10.1007/BFb0053419); [Visual Secret Sharing Schemes](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-71522-9_393).

**Pinned delivered source:** [Shamir Gate 48a7a3ab9298](https://github.com/systemslibrarian/crypto-lab-shamir-gate/tree/48a7a3ab9298edf708f5496159c39f2cdb9d3227).
